estimated median DOR for responders to vimseltinib per RECIST v1.1 was not reached
across all TGCT 30 mg twice weekly cohorts (Cohorts A, B, and 5), with responses
lasting up to 196+ weeks; all responses were ongoing, and most responses were
maintained for at least 12 months.
The updated analysis of MOTION DOR with a median nearly 49 weeks of follow-up was
conducted at a similar median follow-up duration as the Phase 1/2 study (ranged from
48.7 weeks to 56.1 weeks) and similarly demonstrated ongoing responses with
approximately 12 months of on-study treatment. Given participants in Phase 1/2 initiated
treatment before MOTION and had more opportunity to receive treatment past Week 25,
response duration in Phase 1/2 may be considered as an early indicator for patients who
have responded to vimseltinib treatment during the MOTION study.
The DOR per TVS demonstrated a trend similar to those per RECIST v1.1.

ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
Functional and Patient-reported Endpoints
Collectively, assessment of key secondary ROM and PRO endpoints at Week 25 in
MOTION demonstrated statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement
with vimseltinib treatment over placebo (Table 31). The change from baseline in ROM
and PRO endpoints were consistent across the vimseltinib arm in MOTION and Phase
1/2 expansion cohorts.
The Applicant’s Position:
Assessment of the key secondary endpoint ORR by TVS at Week 25 was planned to
provide an early indicator of tumor response with vimseltinib 30 mg twice weekly. The
TVS measures volumetric changes in irregularly shaped tumors like TGCT, which may
not be captured with single dimension measurements per RECIST v1.1 at a given time
point (Tap et al, 2015). Thus, changes in TGCT size may be observed earlier with TVS
than with RECIST v1.1.
ORR per TVS was high in both MOTION vimseltinib arm (67%) and Phase 1/2
expansion Cohort A (51%).
Among objective responders in the vimseltinib arm at Week 25 per RECIST v1.1, the
median DOR was not reached at either the primary or updated analysis with additional 6
months of follow-up, with responses lasting up to 84.4+ weeks and with all but 1
response ongoing at the time of the updated analysis. The updated MOTION DOR data
demonstrate prolonged durability of response in participants with TGCT with further
follow-up.
MOTION met all key secondary endpoints demonstrating statistically significant and
clinically meaningful improvements in TVS, ROM, physical function, worst stiffness,
QoL, and worst pain compared to placebo. The totality of efficacy data demonstrates a
robust, statistically significant, and clinically meaningful efficacy response. The efficacy
and PRO responses in MOTION were consistent with what was observed in participants
with TGCT who received vimseltinib at the recommended dose of 30 mg twice weekly in
the Phase 1/2 study.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA did not independently verify the results of DCC-01-001 presented here, as the
efficacy of vimseltinib was based on MOTION. FDA agrees that based on the data
presented here, ORR per TVS was similar in the MOTION study (67%) and Cohort A of
DCC-3014-01-001.
PRO endpoints in a single-arm study are uninterpretable.

Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
The Applicant’s Position:
There is no formal integrated assessment of efficacy. The data from MOTION, supported
by data from the Phase 1/2 study, demonstrate vimseltinib provides statistically
significant and long-lasting tumor response based on ORR per RECIST v1.1 and ORR by
TVS in participants with symptomatic TGCT. Vimseltinib treatment also resulted in
significant and meaningful improvements based on predetermined MCIDs in function as
measured by active ROM and PROMIS-PF; in symptomatic improvements as measured
by Worst Stiffness NRS and Worst Pain NRS; and in QoL as measured by EQ-VAS.
Responses were observed across subgroups including age, sex, tumor location, and prior
therapies, suggesting generalizability in the intended patient population. The results
support approval of vimseltinib for the treatment of adult patients with symptomatic at
the recommended dose of 30 mg twice weekly.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees that despite no formal integrated assessment of efficacy, the data from
MOTION are statistically and clinically significant to support approval and are consistent
with the results observed in the supportive study DCC-3014-01-001 Cohort A, which was
not independently confirmed by the FDA. The ORR in MOTION per RECIST v1.1
performed at Week 25 shows a clinically significant benefit to patients (MOTION: 40%,
DCC-3014-01-001 Cohort A 30 mg Vimseltinib twice weekly in patients not pre-treated
with anti-CSF1/CSF1R therapy: 38%). In MOTION, the median DOR was not reached,
with a range of 2.5+ to 19.4+ months. A total of 28 (85%) patients experienced a DOR of
6 months and 19 (58%) patients experienced a DOR of 9 months. Additionally,
patients experienced clinically meaningful improvements on key secondary endpoints,
including change from baseline ROM of affected joint and change from baseline in the
PROs (PROMIS-PF and BPI-30 response) which further supported the efficacy endpoint.
Therefore, in addition to inclusion of ORR and DOR in product labeling, the review team
also included results of these secondary endpoints.
The Applicant originally proposed an indication which stated that vimseltinib was
indicated for adult patients with symptomatic TGCT. During review of the NDA
submission, FDA amended the proposed indication to state that it is indicated for the
treatment of patients with symptomatic TGCT for whom surgical resection will
potentially cause worsening functional limitations or severe morbidity which more
accurately reflects the patient population enrolled in MOTION. The Applicant agreed.

These data support the efficacy of vimseltinib in the treatment of patients with TGCT.

Review of Safety
Data:
Refer to Section 8.2.4 for summaries of deaths, SAEs, AEs leading to discontinuations,
and other safety data.
The Applicant’s Position:
Vimseltinib has a manageable and acceptable safety profile in participants with TGCT.
The FDA’s Assessment:
Although, FDA generally agrees with Applicant’s assessment that vimseltinib has an
overall manageable safety profile and an acceptable risk:benefit assessment, a known
adverse reaction with another CSFR1 inhibitor, pexidartinib is hepatotoxicity and
pexidartinib has a BOXED Warning for this toxicity and is only available through a
REMS program.
Cases of serious and fatal liver injury have occurred with the use pexidartinib. Therefore,
FDA conducted a comprehensive review of hepatotoxicity with the use of vimseltinib.
During FDA review, serious and fatal liver injury were not observed with vimseltinib.
Cases of  Grade 2 AST and ALT elevations were common in patients who received
vimseltinib, though no patients developed bilirubin elevations. FDA did not identify any
cases of Hy’s law or drug-induced liver injury (DILI). Due to this known risk of in class
hepatotoxicity, sections 2.2 and 5.1 of product labeling addresses specific guidelines for
managing potential hepatotoxicity in patients, including guidelines for routine laboratory
surveillance.
After multidisciplinary review of clinical and nonclinical data, FDA determined that a
REMS program was not required for vimseltinib. However, FDA will continue to collect
further data on hepatotoxicity through a PMR registry study enrolling at least 100
patients in the postmarketing setting. There is nonclinical evidence to suggest small
molecule inhibitors of CSFR1 lead to a higher incidence of elevated transaminases, but
also may cause hepatic damage. FDA will continue to reevaluate the safety data for
hepatotoxicity.

ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
Additional warnings and precautions included in section 5.1 of product labeling are for
potential embryo-fetal toxicity, allergic reactions to FD&C Yellow No.5 (Tartazine) and
No. 6 (Sunset Yellow FCF) and increased creatinine without affecting renal function.
Safety Review Approach
The Applicant’s Position:
The clinical review of safety of vimseltinib as a treatment for patients with symptomatic
TGCT was based on the MOTION study (details of study design are in Section 8.1.1).
The majority of the safety evaluation is based on the first 25 weeks of treatment to allow
for comparison between the 83 participants randomized to vimseltinib and the 39
participants randomized to placebo.
The integrated safety database comprises data from participants included in the safety
analysis set from the Phase 1/2 study and MOTION, including participants from
MOTION originally randomized to vimseltinib and participants who crossed over to
vimseltinib after receiving placebo. The safety analysis set consists of participants who
have received at least 1 dose of vimseltinib.
The safety analysis set included the following populations:
x

x

x

Pool 1 (All participants with TGCT at 30 mg twice weekly dose): All
participants with TGCT in the Phase 1/2 study and MOTION who received at
least 1 dose of vimseltinib at the recommended dose of 30 mg twice weekly are
included. Data are summarized by Expansion Cohorts and MOTION (Cohort A,
Cohort B, MOTION, Cohort A + MOTION) and overall (Cohort A + Cohort B +
MOTION).
 Phase 1/2 Cohort A is comprised of participants with TGCT who are not
amenable to surgery and who have not received prior CSF1 or CSF1R therapy
(excluding imatinib and/or nilotinib); a population similar to that of the target
patient population of the ongoing pivotal MOTION study. Thus, for the
pooled analysis, Cohort A and MOTION are grouped together.
 Phase 1/2 Cohort B is comprised of participants with TGCT who were not
amenable to surgery and who received prior CSF1 or CSF1R therapy and is
presented separately in the pooled analysis.
Pool 2 (All participants with TGCT, any dose): All participants with TGCT in
the Phase 1/2 study and MOTION who received at least 1 dose of vimseltinib are
included. Data are summarized by study phase (Escalation, Expansion, and
MOTION) and overall.
Pool 3 (All participants, any dose): All participants who received at least 1 dose
of vimseltinib in the Phase 1/2 study and MOTION regardless of tumor type or
dose of vimseltinib are included. Data are summarized by tumor type (MST
versus TGCT) and overall.

Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5532076

4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
x

Pool 4 (All participants at 30 mg twice weekly dose): All participants in the
Phase 1/2 study and MOTION who received at least 1 dose of vimseltinib at the
recommended dose of 30 mg twice weekly, including those participants who
received a loading dose. Data are summarized by tumor type (MST versus TGCT)
and overall.
For all analysis pools, the analysis period comprises the duration from the first dose of
vimseltinib through all available follow-up until the data cutoff (22 Feb 2024 and 27 Dec
2023 for MOTION and the Phase 1/2 study, respectively).
In order to better understand the safety of vimseltinib in the indicated population, a
review of adverse events, laboratory assessments, vital signs, physical findings, and other
observations related to safety was completed for Pool 1. A brief summary of data from
participants in Pool 3 (including participants with MST) was also provided, as this is the
largest safety pool.
In addition, 211 healthy participants received at least one dose of vimseltinib in the
clinical pharmacology studies DCC-3014-01-002, DCC-3014-01-003, DCC-3014-01-004,
and DCC-3014-01-006.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The review team agrees with the safety Pools as stated above were agreed upon in the
preNDA meeting held on January 16, 2024. During the meeting, FDA stated that although
the safety assessment would be based on all patients with any tumor type who received at
least one dose of vimseltinib (Pool 3 n=253), the focus of safety would be on the 83
patients who received vimseltinib 30 mg twice weekly in the double-blind portion of
MOTION. The use of the larger data set was to assess any low incidence occurring serious
adverse events and to contribute to the Warnings and Precautions section of the labeling.
Section 6 of product labeling is thus based on the 83 patients who received vimseltinib in
the double-blind portion of MOTION. Warnings and Precautions evaluated in product
labeling was based on 253 patients who received any dose of vimseltinib in the doubleblind portion of MOTION (n=83), the open-label portion of MOTION (n=35) and in
DCC-3014-01-001 which included patients with TGCT as well as other solid tumors
(n=135). These datasets were independently reviewed by the review team. The safety
analysis within this section will mainly reflect the patients in the double-blind portion of
MOTION unless otherwise stated.
The review team for did not independently evaluate the 211 healthy participants who
received a single-dose of vimseltinib in the clinical pharmacology studies DCC-3014-01002, DCC-3014-01-003, DCC-3014-01-004, and DCC-3014-01-006.

ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position regarding the adequacy of the safety database. The
primary safety analyses to support approval was based on the 83 patients who received
vimseltinib in the double-blind portion of MOTION. An additional 135 patients treated on DCC3014-01-001 and 35 patients who were treated with vimseltinib in the open-label portion of
MOTION were evaluated, for a total of 253 patients who received at least one dose of
vimseltinib.
Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
The Applicant’s Position:
No meaningful concerns are anticipated in the quality and integrity of the submitted datasets and
individual case narratives; these were sufficiently complete to allow for a thorough review of
safety. Furthermore, no data integrity concerns were reported following a completion of site
inspections; data in the case report forms and AE databases were consistent.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with Applicant’s assessment.
Categorization of Adverse Event
The Applicant’s Position:
A TEAE was defined as any AE that occurred or worsened after the administration of the first
dose of vimseltinib and through 30 days after the last dose of vimseltinib or the day before the
start of a new anti-tumor therapy. Drug-related AEs reported after 30 days following the last
dose of vimseltinib were also considered as treatment-emergent.
Adverse events were classified using Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities (MedDRA)
v26.0.
The severity grade of the AEs for the Phase 1/2 study was assessed by National Cancer Institute
Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Event (NCI-CTCAE) v4.03 and for MOTION by
NCI-CTCAE v5.0.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the categorization of TEAEs and severity. The Applicant provided appropriate
definitions and reporting requirements for AEs and serious AEs (SAEs). AE evaluation was
performed at the time of informed consent up to 30 days after the last dose of study treatment.

Routine Clinical Tests
Data:
Routine clinical tests, including vital signs, 12-lead ECG, hematology, chemistry, coagulation,
and urinalysis, were performed according to the Schedules of Assessments in the study protocols
for the MOTION and Phase 1/2 studies.
The Applicant’s Position:
The tests and frequency of testing are considered adequate to assess the safety of vimseltinib in
the MOTION and Phase 1/2 studies.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees that the frequency and type of testing used in these studies was adequate to assess
the safety of vimseltinib.
Safety Results
Deaths
Data:
No participants died in the double-blind period of MOTION.
In Pool 1, one participant in the open-label period of MOTION died from a fall that was assessed
as unrelated to vimseltinib.
In addition, 8 participants with MST in DCC-3014-01-001 experienced TEAEs leading to death.
All were assessed as unrelated to vimseltinib.
The Applicant’s Position:
Vimseltinib was not considered as a cause of death for any reported event.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA reviewed all the death narratives and agrees that vimseltinib was not considered a cause of
death for any event. No deaths are therefore reported in Section 6 of product labeling.

(b) (4)
double-blind period of MOTION;

The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA does not agree with Applicant’s assessment, as all SAEs are considered attributable
regardless of causality. Patients who received vimseltinib experienced more SAEs than those in
the placebo arm (7% vs. 2.6%, respectively) with each SAE occurring in one patient only. FDA
reviewed the narratives for all patients who experienced an SAE. Based on the reviewer
assessment, only cellulitis (1 patient) and sub-cutaneous abscess (1 patient) were considered
SAEs (2.4%). The original proposed labeling from the Applicant did not include SAEs but
through negotiations, an incidence of 2.4% was included in product labeling.

discontinued vimseltinib treatment due to a TEAE compared to 0% in the placebo arm. The
(b) (4)
TEAEs were periorbital oedema,
peripheral sensory neuropathy, rash, and
hypertension. None of these TEAEs led to discontinuation in >1 participant.

Treatment-emergent AEs leading to treatment discontinuation occurred in 9.3% (17/183) of
participants in Pool 1. In Pool 1, the most frequently reported TEAEs leading to treatment
discontinuation were rash and periorbital edema (each reported in 1.6% of participants [3/183]).

The Applicant’s Position:
More vimseltinib-treated participants discontinued study drug treatment than placebo-treated
participants in the double-blind period of MOTION. However, the rate of discontinuation was
still low in vimseltinib-treated participants.
Treatment-emergent AEs leading to treatment discontinuation were generally consistent across
the different pooled populations. TEAEs leading to discontinuation were similar to those that
were managed with interruptions and dose reductions.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA does not agree with applicant’s assessment. A total of 4.8% of patients receiving
vimseltinib discontinued treatment due to a TEAE, including one patient with each of the
following: periorbital edema, peripheral sensory neuropathy, rash and hypertension. FDA does
(b) (4)
not consider
to be an adverse reaction at least possibly attributable to the
use of vimseltinib.
Dose Interruptions, Delays, and/or Reductions Due to Adverse Effects
Data:
In the double-blind period of MOTION, TEAEs leading to dose modification (including dose
reduction or interruption) were experienced by 62.7% of participants in the vimseltinib arm
versus 10.3% in the placebo arm. The most frequently reported TEAEs leading to dose
modification in the vimseltinib arm were periorbital edema (9.6%); COVID-19, pruritus,
asthenia, and blood CPK increased (8.4% each); and rash maculo-papular (7.2%).
In Pool 1, treatment-emergent AEs leading to dose modification occurred in 74.3% of
participants. The most frequently reported TEAEs leading to dose modification were blood
creatine phosphokinase (CPK) increased (16.9% [31/183]), periorbital edema (13.7% [25/183]),
asthenia (12.0% [22/183]), pruritus (9.8% [18/183]), rash maculo-papular (9.3% [17/183]), and
COVID-19 (8.2% [15/183]).
Similar to Pool 1, in Pool 3 the most frequently reported TEAEs leading to dose modification
were blood CPK increased (14.3% [36/251]), periorbital edema (12.0% [30/251]), and asthenia
(8.8% [22/251]).
The Applicant’s Position:
198

ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
TEAEs leading to dose modification were generally consistent across the different pooled
populations and were similar to those seen in the double-blind period of MOTION. Although
most participants experienced a TEAE leading to dose modification, the RDI was high (75%),
as discussed in Section 8.1.2
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA analyzed the dose interruptions or reductions in patients on the double-blind portion of
MOTION. Dose reductions due to an adverse reaction occurred in 39% of patients who received
vimseltinib. Adverse reactions leading to dose reductions in 2% of patients were rash,
periorbital edema, peripheral edema, fatigue, pruritus, face edema, increased CPK, neuropathy,
and hypertension. Dose interruptions due to adverse reaction abnormality occurred in 40% of
patients who received vimseltinib. Adverse reactions leading to interruptions in 2% of patients
included rash, fatigue, peripheral edema, increased CPK, periorbital edema, face edema, pruritus,
neuropathy, and hypertension. The Applicant’s statement above that “TEAEs leading to dose
modification (including dose reduction or interruption) were experienced by 62.7% of
participants in the vimseltinib” includes overlap between some patients who experienced both
dose reductions and dose interruptions.

Drug-related TEAEs
In the double-blind period of MOTION, drug-related TEAEs with 5% higher incidence with
vimseltinib than placebo were periorbital edema, fatigue, face edema, asthenia, pruritus, blood
CPK increased, AST increased, rash, rash maculo-papular, peripheral edema, hypertension, and
eyelid edema.
In Pool 1, the drug-related TEAEs reported in 25% of participants were periorbital edema
(44.8% [82/183]), blood CPK increased (42.1% [77/183]), fatigue (28.4% [52/183]), asthenia
(27.9% [51/183]), headache (27.3% [50/183]), AST increased (26.2% [48/183]), pruritus (26.2%
[48/183]), and face edema (25.1% [46/183]).
Similar to Pool 1, in Pool 3 the drug-related TEAEs reported in 25% of participants were
periorbital edema (42.2% [106/251]), blood CPK increased (40.2% [101/251]), and fatigue
(29.1% [73/251]).

ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
Grade 3/4 TEAEs
In the double-blind period of MOTION, Grade 3/4 TEAEs were experienced by 37.3% in the
vimseltinib arm and 10.3% in the placebo arm. Grade 3/4 TEAEs experienced by >1 participant
in the vimselinib arm included blood CPK increased, hypertension, periorbital edema, pruritus,
and asthenia.
In Pool 1, 53.0% (97/183) of participants reported Grade 3/4 TEAEs. The most frequently
reported Grade 3/4 TEAEs were blood CPK increased (23.5% [43/183]), hypertension (8.7%
[16/183]), and pruritus (2.7% [5/183]).
Similar to Pool 1, in Pool 3, 53.8% (135/251) of participants reported Grade 3/4 TEAEs. The
most frequently reported Grade 3/4 TEAEs were blood CPK increased (21.1% [53/251]),
hypertension (7.2% [18/251]), and AST increased, lipase increased, and fatigue (2.4% [6/251]
each)
Adverse Drug Reactions
All reported TEAEs from both MOTION and the Phase 1/2 study were evaluated to identify
those that were reasonably associated with the use of vimseltinib. Adverse drug reactions were
identified based on event incidence, difference between the vimseltinib and placebo arms in
MOTION, temporal relationship, seriousness, severity, consistency across studies, nonclinical
findings, and plausible mechanism of action.
Placebo-controlled data from the double-blind period of MOTION (DCO date 22 August 2023)
were selected as the best available data for the presentation of adverse drug reactions in the label.
Participants were counted once for each adverse drug reaction term, and incidence rates were
based on the number of participants who initially received placebo or vimseltinib.
Table 47 summarizes the adverse reactions observed in MOTION through Week 25. Events that
were coded under different terms in the database but that represent the same phenomenon were
grouped together as a single adverse drug reaction. Preferred terms included within each grouped
term are captured in the footnotes.

Laboratory Findings
Data:
Chemistry
During the double-blind period of MOTION, notable differences between vimseltinib and
placebo were observed for alanine aminotransferase (ALT), AST, Alkaline phosphatase (ALP),
creatinine, and cholesterol. The majority of shifts in serum chemistry parameters from baseline
to worst post-baseline CTCAE Grade were normal to Grade 1. No other clinically meaningful
abnormalities in serum chemistry parameters were observed.
Across all pools, the most frequently reported shifts in chemistry laboratory parameters
(worsening from baseline in 20% of participants) were ALT increased, AST increased,
cholesterol high, creatinine increased, hypertriglyceridemia, and hypoglycemia. Most of these
reported shifts were worsening to <Grade 3.
In Pool 1, the parameters that showed any shift in 20% of participants were ALT increased
(27.2% [50/184]), AST increased (92.4% [170/184]), cholesterol high (52.7% [97/184])
206
Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5532076

4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
creatinine increased (42.9% [79/184]), hypertriglyceridemia (27.2% [50/184]), and
hypoglycemia (22.8% [42/184]). Of these, the parameters that showed worsening to Grade 3/4
were AST increased (0.5% [1/184]), cholesterol high (1.1% [2/184]), and hypertriglyceridemia
(1.1% [2/184]).
Although ALT or AST increases were frequently observed in participants who received
vimseltinib, a small proportion of elevations were 3× upper limit of normal (ULN). None of
these elevations were accompanied by 1×ULN of total bilirubin. No Hy’s Law cases were
identified.
Similarly, the cholestatic screening plot does not suggest any occurrence of cholestatic drug
induced liver injury. Although an increase in ALP >2×ULN occurred in 2 participants, this
increase was not accompanied by an increase in total bilirubin.
Results were similar in Pool 3.
Hematology
During the double-blind period of MOTION, notable differences between vimseltinib and
placebo shifts in hematology parameters from baseline to worst post-baseline CTCAE Grade
were reported for leukocytes (overall) and neutrophils. No other clinically meaningful shifts in
hematology parameters from baseline to worst post-baseline CTCAE Grade were reported in the
vimseltinib treatment period.
In Pool 1, the parameters that showed any worsening in 25% of participants were neutrophil
count decreased (36.4% [67/184]) and white blood cell decreased (33.2% [61/184]). Of these,
few participants showed worsening to Grade 3/4 (2.7% [5/184] of participants with neutrophil
count decreased and 0.5% [1/184] of participants with white blood cell decreased).
All observed laboratory abnormalities from both MOTION and pooled data were evaluated to
identify those that were reasonably associated with the use of vimseltinib. Considering event
incidence, difference between the vimseltinib and placebo arms in MOTION, temporal
relationship, seriousness, severity, consistency across studies, nonclinical findings, and plausible
mechanism of action, laboratory abnormalities observed through Week 25 in MOTION and
considered reasonably associated with the use of vimseltinib are summarized in Table 49.

In addition, increased CPK was identified as a laboratory abnormality reasonably associated with
the use of vimseltinib. The frequency of increased CPK cannot be determined from MOTION
because CPK was not assessed at baseline. In the Phase 1/2 study conducted in 65 participants
receiving vimseltinib 30 mg twice weekly, increased CPK from baseline was observed in all
participants. Skeletal muscle injury or other organ damage was not observed in patients with
elevated CPK. There were no treatment discontinuations due to increased CPK reported in
MOTION or the Phase 1/2 study.
The Applicant’s Position:
Serum enzyme elevations are frequently observed in patients treated with CSF1R inhibitors,
which are known to reduce Kupffer cells in the liver as part of the mechanism of action. None of
the changes met Hy’s Law criteria and there was no evidence of cholestatic hepatotoxicity,
skeletal muscle injury, or other organ damage.
Decreased neutrophils were observed with vimseltinib treatment during double-blind period of
MOTION but were not associated with symptoms.
The FDA’s Assessment:
In general, the most clinically important laboratory abnormalities appear to be increases in liver
enzymes, which occurred without associated elevations in bilirubin, as shown in lab shift table,
Table 50. Overall, the worsening in baseline of AST or ALT elevation (92% and 24%,
respectively) in patients treated with vimseltinib was higher than in patients treated with placebo
(10% and 15%, respectively). No patients experienced a Grade 3 or higher elevation in AST or
ALT in the vimseltinib arm. Refer to Section 8.2.5 for further discussion and overall conclusions
regarding hepatotoxicity risk with vimselinib.

Vital Signs
Data:
In Pool 1, the mean (STD) baseline systolic and diastolic blood pressures for all participants
were 123.9 (13.01) and 75.3 (9.38) mmHg, respectively. A total of 38.6% (71/184) of
participants experienced no shift in post-baseline systolic blood pressure (BP) during treatment,
and 10.9% (20/184) of participants experienced a maximum shift in systolic BP from baseline
from <140 to 160 mmHg. A total of 46.2% (85/184) of participants experienced no shift in
post-baseline diastolic BP, and 6.5% (12/184) of participants experienced a maximum shift in
diastolic BP from baseline from <90 to 100 mmHg.
Results for Pool 3 were similar.
No other clinically meaningful changes from baseline in vital sign measurements were observed.
The Applicant’s Position:
Hypertension was identified as an adverse drug reaction of vimseltinib. Hypertension can be
managed in clinical practice and is not an important safety concern for vimseltinib. No other
clinically meaningful changes from baseline in vital sign measurements were observed.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with Applicant’s assessment. Median baseline systolic blood pressure for the 83
patients who received vimseltinib was 124.1 (range: 100, 180) and the mean change from
baseline at week 25 was +5.9 (range: -16, +36). Median diastolic blood pressure for the 83
patients who received vimseltinib was 76 (range: 51, 96) and the mean change from baseline at
week 25 was +5 (range: -17, +24).
Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
Data:
During the double-blind period in MOTION, 1 participant in the vimseltinib arm had postbaseline QTcF >450 ms, and 13 (15.9%) participants had QTcF increase from baseline >30 ms.

None of the worst post-baseline ECG changes in the vimseltinib arm was interpreted as clinically

In Pool 1, 15.4% (28/184) of participants experienced a QTcF increase from baseline >30 ms and
1.1% (2/184) of participants experienced a QTcF increase from baseline >60 ms.
In Pool 3, 14.9% (36/253) of participants experienced a QTcF increase from baseline >30 ms and

No participant experienced QTcF prolongation >500 ms and only 3 participants experienced
QTcF prolongation above the baseline >60 ms.
No participant experienced QTcF prolongation on multiple occasions. No observation was
considered clinically significant, and none led to dose modification or treatment discontinuation.

Vimseltinib administration was not associated with clinically significant postbaseline changes in
ECG parameters.
Based on the integrated analysis, vimseltinib did not appear to have an effect on the QTcF
interval.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s assessment. One patient each in the placebo and vimseltinib
arms experienced a post-baseline QTcF of >450 ms. In the vimseltinib arm, 13 patients (16%)
experienced a QTcF increase of >30 ms. In the placebo arm, 7 patients (18%) experienced a
QTcF increase of >30 ms. These were not assessed as clinically relevant.
QT
Data:
A concentration QTc modeling analysis was performed based on the data from participants with
MST in the Phase 1/2 study and healthy participants from Study DCC-3014-01-002 (who are
physiologically similar to participants with TGCT). The results from the cardiodynamic
evaluation of the 40 mg and 50 mg doses demonstrated that vimseltinib has no clinically relevant
effect on heart rate or on cardiac conduction (the PR and QRS intervals). There was no clinically
meaningful effect on the QTc interval, and an effect on ¨¨QTcF exceeding 10 ms can be
excluded within the observed ranges of plasma concentrations of vimseltinib, up to 1774 ng/mL.
The Applicant’s Position:
At exposures exceeding 2 times the mean maximum exposure of the recommended dosage,
vimseltinib does not prolong the QTc interval to any clinically relevant extent.
The FDA’s Assessment:

Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
The Applicant’s Position:
Adverse drug reactions and laboratory abnormalities are discussed above. No additional
submission-specific safety issues have been identified.
The FDA’s Assessment:
As previously mentioned, worsening in baseline of AST or ALT elevation (92% and 24%,
respectively) in patients treated with vimseltinib was higher than in patients treated with placebo
(10% and 15%, respectively) however no patients experienced a Grade 3 or higher elevation in
AST or ALT in the vimseltinib arm and there were no bilirubin elevations, Hy’s Law cases or
other signs of severe liver toxicity in the submitted safety datasets for MOTION.
Nonetheless, the risk of hepatotoxicity or liver injury was identified as serious risk during the
development of pexidartinib which shares the same target; CSFR1. Pexidartinib was the first
CSF1R targeting inhibitor that was FDA-approved for systemic use in patients with TGCT.
Pexidartinib was approved based on the clinical trial ENLIVEN, which treated 61 patients, and a
total of 768 patients received pexidartinib during all clinical trials for which data was available at
the time of pexidartinib approval.
To enroll on ENLIVEN, patients were required to have total bilirubin and AST/ALT 1.5 times
the upper limit of normal (ULN). In ENLIVEN, serum transaminase elevations occurred in a
majority of patients; elevations in ALT and AST occurred in 67% and 90% of patients,
respectively; bilirubin increases occurred in 12% of patients. Grade 3 or higher elevations in
ALT and AST occurred in 20% and 12% of patients, respectively. Grade 3 or higher bilirubin
elevation occurred in 3.3%. Concerningly, approximately 5% of patients in the ENLIVEN trial
experienced a pattern of serum transaminase and bilirubin elevation indicative of severe liver
injury, characterized by AST or ALT greater than 3 times the ULN with concurrent bilirubin
increases greater than 2 times the ULN.

212
Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5532076

4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
Among the 768 patients in clinical trials, there were two cases of irreversible cholestatic liver
injury; one patient with advanced cancer and ongoing liver toxicity died and one patient with a
confirmed case of VBDS required a liver transplant. The review team thus instituted a boxed
warning in product labeling for pexidartinib regarding serious and potentially fatal liver injury,
and pexidartinib was made available only via the risk evaluation and mitigation (REMS)
program with an Elements to Assure Safe Use (ETASU). The REMS program (n=609) revealed
similar numbers observed of serious liver injury, with 32 patients (5.3%) developing liver
toxicity within 71 days of the first dose of pexidartinib; ten required hospitalization, and two
developing VBDS.
Because of the known risk of hepatotoxicity with pexidartinib as described above, FDA reviewed
vimseltinib’s NDA carefully for evidence of similar toxicity and evaluated the possible need for
a REMS and/or a boxed warning in product labeling. The evaluation of hepatoxicity for
vimseltinib was primarily based on MOTION but also includes data collected from DCC-301401-001 and is described below.
Hepatotoxicity in MOTION
In the original protocol for MOTION, the risk of hepatotoxicity was mitigated with selection of
patients without baseline liver toxicity, laboratory and clinical monitoring for liver toxicity,
dosage interruptions and management guidelines for hepatobiliary laboratory abnormalities.
Patients treated on MOTION had AST, ALT and bilirubin assessed prior to treatment initiation,
and then at day 1 and 15 of the first cycle, and the first day of each 28-day cycle thereafter.
Patients treated on ENLIVEN, the pivotal study of pexidartinib, had a similar evaluation
schedule, with ALT, AST and bilirubin assessed prior to initiation of treatment, weekly during
cycle 1, and then once per 28-day cycle thereafter. It is unlikely that clinically significant LFT
elevation events would have been missed in MOTION, as the monitoring schedule was fairly
rigorous.
Table 51 lists all observed cases of laboratory abnormalities related to liver function in patients
enrolled in MOTION. While AST and ALT elevations from baseline were common, almost all
were Grade 1 in severity with only 3.6% experiencing Grade 2 events of AST increase, and no
Grade 3 or higher events observed in any patients. There were no dose reductions or
discontinuations due to liver function elevations and no SAEs related to hepatotoxicity.

Hepatotoxicity in the overall clinical development program (Pool 3)
Additional analyses that include all 253 patients in Pool 3 are summarized below. This pool
includes patients with solid tumors who may have had liver metastases and elevated liver
chemistries at baseline. Overall, despite more liberal inclusion criteria in the broader database,
very few Grade 3 events of AST elevation (2.4%) or ALT elevation (0.8%) were observed, with
no Grade 3 bilirubin elevation and no Hy’s Law cases.

ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
Source: FDA generated table

In terms of timing and resolution of observed transaminase elevations, the median time to initial
increase in AST/ALT was 2.1 weeks (range: 1.0-72.3) and 4.1 weeks (range 1.0, 151.6),
respectively. Median time to initial increase in bilirubin was 16.1 (range 1.0-126.3) weeks. These
data point to early onset of these mild transaminase increases, with median time to onset
occurring within the first cycle of study drug. In Pool 3 (n=253), 26 patients had Grade 2 AST
or ALT elevations. Of these, 21 patients returned to Grade 1 or baseline. Of the 5 remaining
patients that did not return to Grade 1 or baseline, the elevations did not worsen beyond Grade 2.
The median time to resolution to Grade 1 or baseline for Grade 2 ALT or AST elevation was
3.3 weeks.
In terms of reported management of these LFT elevations, among the 37 patients in Pool 3 with a
reported AE of ALT increased, 2 patients experienced a dose modification (interruption and/or
dose reduction) and none withdrew treatment due to ALT increased. Among the 66 patients in
Pool 3 with a reported AE of AST increased, 9 experienced a dose modification and 1 withdrew
treatment due to AST increased. There were only 2 patients with AEs of blood bilirubin
increased, which did not lead to a dose modification or discontinuation. Among the 10 patients
with dose interruptions due to an AE of ALT/AST increased, 9 were rechallenged, with 5
patients experiencing recurrent AEs of ALT/AST increased of the same or lower grade (positive
rechallenge).
Review of the overall safety database was generally concordant with the data from patients
treated in MOTION and did not raise concern that clinically significant hepatotoxicity had
occurred across the larger development program, despite inclusion of some patients with liver
metastases and/ or mildly elevated liver chemistries at baseline.
Overall Assessment
Given the relatively limited number of patients treated with vimseltinib and the known severe,
irreversible liver toxicity observed with pexidartinib, FDA considered the need for a boxed
warning or REMS program to ensure the safe use of vimseltinib. The primary review team
discussed the safety findings and need for a REMS or boxed warning informally with FDA’s
Division of Risk Management (DRM), the Office of Oncologic Diseases (OOD) safety team, and
the labeling team during the course of the evaluation of liver toxicity.
After extensive multidisciplinary discussions, review of nonclinical findings, presented safety
data, and other literature, the review team determined that there is no current evidence of severe
hepatotoxicity in patients treated with vimseltinib. Unlike in the pexidartinib development
program, for patients treated with vimseltinib, there were no cases of Grade 3 or higher
elevations in AST or ALT on MOTION, no cases of Hy’s law or DILI, no patients required
discontinuation of treatment due to elevated AST/ALT and no patients developed symptomatic
liver toxicity. A total of 253 patients were treated with vimseltinib and there were no reported
cases of liver injury. ENLIVEN, the registrational trial for pexidartinib, exhibited much more
215
Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5532076

4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
concerning evidence for hepatotoxicity, for example with Grade 3 or higher elevations in ALT in
20% for patients and Hy’s Law cases in 5% of patients. If vimseltinib was truly associated with
concerning hepatotoxicity to the same extent, a similar percentage of patients would likely have
exhibited evidence of hepatotoxicity in MOTION. In contrast, there were no Grade 3 elevations
or HY’s Law cases in MOTION, which is reassuring regarding the likely absence of severe risk
for hepatotoxicity with vimseltinib.
In discussion regarding the use of a boxed warning, the review team did not find evidence to
suggest that there was a risk of serious, fatal, or life-threatening consequences that would require
alerting patients and providers in an enhanced manner. Regarding a discussion of the need for a
REMS, given the low-grade events, the review team did not feel that patient selection, careful
monitoring, avoiding, certain concomitant therapy, addition of another drug or managing patients
in a manner, or avoiding use in a specific clinical situation was required for administration of
vimseltinib. Although class labeling could be considered based on the known risk of
pexidartinib, the safety signal for hepatotoxicity was much higher and more severe with the
available clinical trial data on pexidartinib at the time of approval action compared with the data
available at the time of approval for vimseltinib.
The Applicant did not originally propose to include any mention of hepatotoxicity in product
labeling for vimseltinib. To further clarify the Applicant’s stance, FDA sent an information
request to the Applicant on October 24, 2025, requesting additional information to support the
Applicant’s position that hepatotoxicity with pexidartinib does not reflect a class effect and that
no labelled warning was required for safe use of vimseltinib. The Applicant provided review of
published literature that suggested that the molecular structure of pexidartinib undergoes
extensive metabolism involving the formation of reactive intermediates with potential to modify
biological macromolecules. The Applicant argued that formation of such reactive species has
previously been associated with drug induced liver injury (DILI) and overt hepatotoxicity in
humans across a range of chemotypes and pharmacological mechanisms unrelated to CSF1R
inhibition. The mechanism of hepatotoxicity may be related to metabolism of the pyrrole ring of
pexidartinib by CYP3A-mediated oxidation, which is not generalizable to all CSF1R inhibitors
and non-clinical data via published literature suggested that pexidartinib-induced hepatotoxicity
may be due to mitochondrial injury. The Applicant thus argued that this mechanism of
hepatotoxicity was specific to pexidartinib and was not relevant to vimseltinib. While the FDA
review team, including nonclinical pharmacology/ toxicology, reviewed the referenced literature
and thought that the argument advanced by the Applicant might be plausible, the team concluded
that the risk of hepatotoxicity related to agents targeting CSF1R could not be fully ruled out
based on clinical and non-clinical data (See Section 5).
Given that there is some degree of residual uncertainty of the potential for hepatotoxicity based
on the reviewed vimseltinib data given its relatively small safety database, enrollment of patients
with no baseline hepatic liver abnormalities or disease on MOTION, observed low-grade LFT
elevations in the majority of patients, and unclear definitive mechanism of hepatotoxicity with
the CSF1R inhibitor pexidartinib, the review team determined that a Warnings and Precautions
216
Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
for hepatotoxicity was warranted. Within the warning, the review team included a statement to
avoid use of vimseltinib in patients with pre-existing increased serum transaminases, total
bilirubin or direct bilirubin (>ULN), or active liver or biliary tract disease, including ALP. This
is consistent with inclusion criteria for MOTION. In addition, the review team added
recommended monitoring for LFTs to product labeling, including AST, ALT, total bilirubin,
direct bilirubin, ALP and gamma-glutamyl transferase (GGT), prior to initiation of vimseltinib,
twice a month for the first two months and once every 3 months for the first year of therapy and
as clinically indicated thereafter. This schedule was instituted accounting for the observation that
toxicity generally occurs early in the administration of pexidartinib, with most (96%) of cases of
pexidartinib-induced liver injury occurring in the first two months of administration, thus
presumably any cases of liver injury occurring with vimseltinib would be accounted for with the
recommended schedule. The review team also added a table of recommended dose modifications
for vimseltinib in cases of elevated AST, ALT, bilirubin, INR, or ALP.
To further assess the potential risk for hepatotoxicity, a PMR (see section 13) was issued that
will include a patient registry to provide additional information to further characterize the risk of
hepatotoxicity in the indicated population outside of the clinical trial setting and to elucidate the
potential long-term risk of liver injury associated with vimseltinib treatment. In addition, an
enhanced pharmacovigilance program will be instituted to capture patients who may not be
included in the PMR registry.

Human Reproduction and Pregnancy
Data:
No clinical studies have been performed to assess use of vimseltinib during pregnancy or
lactation.
In animals, malformations of the cardiovascular and skeletal systems along with additional
indications of developmental perturbation, including structural anatomic variations and
indications of developmental delay, occurred at 15 mg/kg/day (~20 times the exposure at the
recommended human dose). There were no adverse findings in the pregnant animals and the
maternal NOAEL (no adverse effect level) was 15 mg/kg/day, the highest dose evaluated. Due to
the adverse malformations in the fetuses at 15 mg/kg/day, the developmental NOAEL was
5 mg/kg/day.
One pregnancy was reported in the Phase 1/2 study. One additional pregnancy was reported in a
female partner of a male participant in the open-label period in MOTION. No evidence of birth

Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound
The Applicant’s Position:
Based on the mechanism of action, drug abuse, withdrawal, or rebound effects are not
anticipated with vimseltinib. There have been no cases of treatment overdose reported in any
clinical study of vimseltinib. Treatment of overdose consists of observation and general
supportive measures.

FDA agrees with this statement.

Safety in the Postmarket Setting
Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
The Applicant’s Position:
Not applicable. Vimseltinib has not been marketed in any country.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with this statement.
Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting
The Applicant’s Position:
Very common and common toxicities appear to have been adequately represented in the studies
with vimseltinib. Potential safety concerns with uncommon or rare occurrence may be detected
during the postmarketing period. Routine pharmacovigilance will be conducted to monitor for
these adverse events.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA does not agree. Because uncertainty exists with respect to hepatotoxicity, a postmarketing
requirement will be issued to assess the potential risk of severe hepatotoxicity. (See Section 13).
An enhanced pharmacovigilance plan to collect data on events in the postmarketing setting for
patients not captured in the registry was instituted.
Integrated Assessment of Safety
Data:
A total of 253 participants constituted the vimseltinib safety database from the MOTION and
Phase 1/2 studies, of whom 216 were participants with TGCT. The safety profile of participants
with TGCT enrolled in the Phase 1/2 study were similar to those enrolled in MOTION.
In Pool 1 of the integrated analysis (184 participants), the mean (STD) duration of exposure to
vimseltinib was 14.5 months (9.28).
Most participants (99.5%) reported at least 1 TEAE. The most frequently reported (25% of
participants) TEAEs in participants receiving vimseltinib were periorbital edema (45.4%), blood
CPK increased (42.6%), and headache (33.9%). Of these, most were low grade in severity,
nonserious, and considered drug-related.
The most frequently reported Grade 3/4 TEAEs were blood CPK increased (23.5%),
hypertension (8.7%), and pruritus (2.7%).
220
Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5532076

4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
Serious AEs were reported in 18.0% of participants. The most frequently reported SAEs were
cellulitis (2.7%) and fall (1.1%). Most SAEs were considered unrelated to vimseltinib, and no
related SAEs were experienced by >1 participant.
Treatment-emergent AEs leading to dose modification (reduction or interruption) were reported
in 74.3% of participants. The most frequently reported TEAEs leading to dose modification were
blood CPK increased (16.9%), periorbital edema (13.7%), asthenia (12.0%), pruritus (9.8%),
rash maculo-papular (9.3%), and COVID19 (8.2%).
Treatment-emergent AEs leading to treatment discontinuation were reported in 9.3% of
participants. The most frequently reported TEAEs leading to treatment discontinuation were rash
and periorbital edema (each reported in 1.6% of participants).
Review of all TEAEs occurring in Years 2 through 4 did not reveal an increased incidence of
events typically occurring with long latency. No new or worsening cardiovascular toxicities,
secondary malignancies, renal or hepatic toxicities, or other clinically significant AEs were
observed with long-term vimseltinib exposure.
No drug-related events leading to death occurred in the entire safety population.
Serum enzyme elevations including ALT, AST, ALP, and CPK were observed. None of the
changes met Hy’s Law criteria and there was no evidence of cholestatic hepatotoxicity, skeletal
muscle injury, or other organ damage.
Decreased neutrophils were observed but were not associated with symptoms.
Overall, vimseltinib was generally safe with a manageable AE profile in participants with TGCT.
The Applicant’s Position:
The evaluation of the safety data from participants with TGCT who received vimseltinib
treatment at the recommended dose of 30 mg twice weekly in MOTION and Phase 1/2
demonstrated an acceptable safety profile.
Most of the TEAEs observed were nonserious, Grade 1 or Grade 2 in severity, and manageable
in clinical practice.
The FDA’s Assessment:
In general, FDA agrees with Applicant’s assessment. FDA reviewed the following:
x 83 patients who received vimseltinib in MOTION at the proposed dose.
x 218 patients who were treated in clinical studies, including the 83 patients in MOTION
and included 135 in DCC-2013-01-001, and
x 253 patients including the 218 above and 35 patients who received vimseltinib in the
open-label period.
FDA specifically reviewed data regarding hepatotoxicity and noted no cases of Hy’s law in this

safety population. Refer to section 5.1 of product labeling for additional information. In review

Patients treated on MOTION had AST, ALT and bilirubin assessed prior to treatment initiation,

Treatment with vimseltinib resulted in frequent AST and ALT elevations, without Hy’s law or
evidence of other liver dysfunction, and after extensive multidisciplinary review of the safety
data, nonclinical findings and literature, it is FDA’s opinion that there is no evidence to date of
severe hepatotoxicity in patients treated with vimseltinib. However, post-marketing monitoring
will be required, given the small sample size treated.

SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
Statistical Issues
The FDA’s Assessment:
The MOTION trial demonstrated a statistically significant improvement in ORR, BPI-30
response rates, mean change from baseline to Week 25 in active ROM, and PROMIS-PF. The
robustness of results was established based on extensive sensitivity analysis corresponding to
each of these endpoints as presented in the “Study results” section of this review.
The results of the key secondary endpoint of ORR at Week 25 per TVS was statistically
significant; however, this endpoint has not been validated as a surrogate for any of the
established endpoints for clinical benefit claims in oncology, and therefore FDA considers ORR
by TVS as supportive.
FDA concludes that MOTION demonstrated a statistically significant improvement in ROM for
vimseltinib compared to placebo. The magnitude of improvement (difference in LS means) from
baseline for patients on the vimseltinib arm compared to placebo arm was estimated to be 14.6%
(4.0, 25.3). Although a treatment benefit was demonstrated for ROM, interpretation of the effect
is unclear due to limited information on clinical meaningfulness for the target patient population
in study MOTION. Based on the exploratory responder analysis using Applicant’s proposed
clinically meaningful threshold of 10%, there appears to be clinical benefit of vimseltinib on
active ROM because more patients in the vimseltinib arm (48%) had a clinically meaningful
improvement in ROM in the affected joints compared to the placebo arm (20%).
The results for 15-item PROMIS-PF in vimseltinib arm were statistically significant compared to
placebo arm (Difference in LS means for change from baseline to Week 25: 3.3 [95% CI: 1.4,
5.2]). Due to limited information provided by the Applicant for evaluation of proposed clinical
meaningful change, a definitive conclusion regarding the clinically meaningful difference cannot
be made. However, based on FDA’s assessment, a plausible clinical meaningful change may
range from 3 to 7-points, and the observed effect of 3.3 points was covered by the range.
BPI-30 response rate was significantly different between the arms. Additional sensitivity
analyses were performed to assess the robustness of the observed results and also beyond the
Applicant specified definition. The sensitivity analyses support the primary analyses results and
concludes that the there is a substantial improvement(decrease) in the pain experienced by the
patients treated with vimseltinib when compared to placebo.
Results from the Worst Stiffness NRS and EQ-VAS are not considered as part of improvement
in quality-of-life assessments.

ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)

Conclusions and Recommendations
The FDA’s Assessment:
Based on the totality of evidence, the multidisciplinary review teams recommend granting
regular approval of vimseltinib 30 mg BIW for the treatment of adult patients with symptomatic
TGCT for which surgical resection will potentially cause worsening functional limitation or
severe morbidity.
The Applicant submitted substantial evidence of safety and effectiveness from the pivotal
MOTION Study to support approval. MOTION demonstrated that vimseltinib provided
clinically meaningful and statistically significant improvement in ORR. The ORR was also
statistically different between the two arms with 40% (95% CI: 29, 51) and 0% (95% CI: 0, 9) in
the vimseltinib, and placebo arms, respectively. The responses were durable with a median
duration that was NR in the vimseltinib arm with a range from 2.5+ to 19.4+ months. Of the 33
patients who treated with vimseltinib who experienced an objective response, duration of
response was 6 months for 85% of patients and 9 months for 58%. These results were further
supported by improvements in ROM and clinical outcome assessment data of patient-reported
pain, and patient-reported physical functioning data.
In general, the safety findings were consistent with the known adverse reactions commonly seen
with the use of other CSFR1 inhibitors. The reviewer did not find evidence of severe or lifethreatening hepatotoxicity with the use of vimseltinib at the proposed dose in an overall small
sample size. Low-Grade elevation of AST and/or ALT were noted without a concurrent increase
in bilirubin. There were no deaths, or cases of Hy’s law or DILI. A Warnings and Precautions
and dose modifications for hepatotoxicity were added to the USPI for vimseltinib. A PMR and
enhanced pharmacovigilance plane for further assessment of hepatotoxicity were issued.

10 Pediatrics
The Applicant’s Position:
On 12 March 2021, the Sponsor submitted an iPSP requesting a full drug-specific waiver for
vimseltinib for the treatment of symptomatic TGCT not amendable to surgery for all pediatric
(b)
age groups (0 < (4) years) on the basis that clinical studies would be impossible or highly
impracticable to conduct in the pediatric population. On 16 July 2021, FDA issued an Agreed
iPSP Agreement letter for a full waiver for vimseltinib in all subsets of the pediatric population.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees that a full wavier for vimseltinib in the pediatric population is appropriate.

12 Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategies (REMS)
The FDA’s Assessment:
Pexidartinib was approved by the FDA for patients with TGCT associated with severe morbidity
or functional limitations and not amenable to surgery. However, in the pivotal study to support
approval, ENLIVEN, 3 of 61 (5%) patients who received pexidartinib developed signs of serious
liver injury. Hepatotoxicity including liver failure and life-threatening vanishing bile duct
syndrome (VBDS), ductopenia, and symptomatic cholestasis can occur and can occur despite
monitoring and prompt drug cessation. Thus, pexidartinib has a boxed warning for hepatotoxicity
and a REMS mitigate the risk of serious and potentially fatal liver injury. Given the important
identified risk of severe hepatotoxicity observed with the use of pexidartinib, the review team
performed a comprehensive analysis of the risk of hepatotoxicity for vimseltinib. The review
team considered the need for a REMS based on a class effect and the identified risk for
hepatotoxicity with use of pexidartinib. However, the safety data from the 253 patients who
received at least one dose of vimseltinib in MOTION or the supportive study, DCC-3014-01001, did not identify a risk of serious or significant hepatotoxicity that would require a REMS.
While transient AST and ALT elevations were common in MOTION, there were no cases of
bilirubin elevation and no cases of Hy’s law.
The safe use of vimseltinib can be adequately implemented in the postmarketing setting without
issuing a REMS for this drug product. The product labeling for vimseltinib includes information
on common and clinically significant adverse reactions that have been observed across the drug
class including hepatotoxicity, and trained oncologists have experience in the monitoring and
management of these adverse reactions. Product labeling also includes dose modification and
management guidelines any events as well as specific guidelines for hepatotoxicity. Risk
management based on labeling, routine pharmacovigilance, and a PMR for hepatotoxicity is
expected to ensure the safe use of vimseltinib.

13 Postmarketing Requirements and Commitment
The FDA’s Assessment:
The following postmarketing requirement with milestone goal dates were issued to the
Applicant.
Clinical
4791-2 Conduct a prospective clinical registry to evaluate the potential risk of serious
hepatotoxicity in patients receiving vimseltinib for the treatment of tenosynovial giant cell
tumor. Analyses should include evaluation of alanine aminotransferase, aspartate
aminotransferase, bilirubin, symptoms suggestive of hepatotoxicity, and dose reduction and
treatment discontinuation related to hepatotoxicity. Include investigations of time to event and
severity, and potential risk factors such as age, race, and comorbidities. The registry should aim
to enroll at least 100 patients. Monitor patients until discontinuation of study treatment or a
minimum of 5 years from start of treatment, whichever occurs first. Interim safety analyses
should be performed yearly beginning one year after the first patient has received vimseltinib to
characterize the long-term risk of hepatotoxicity with vimseltinib.

OCP Appendices (Technical documents supporting OCP
recommendations)
Population PK Analysis
19.4.1.1.

Executive Summary

The FDA’s Assessment:
The PK of vimseltinib in adult population was characterized by a 2-compartment
model with sequential zero- and first-order absorption followed by first-order
elimination. The population PK model described the observed data reasonably well.
The proposed dosage of 30 mg biw is considered acceptable for the proposed patient
population.
19.4.1.2.

PPK Assessment Summary

The Applicant’s Position:
[Highlight the key findings in the white cells.]
General Information
Objectives of PPK Analysis

Studies Included
Dose(s) Included

The aim of this analysis was to characterize the population PK of vimseltinib in
healthy volunteers, patients with malignant solid tumors, and patients with
TGCT.
DCC-3014-01-001, DCC-3014-01-002, and DCC-3014-03-001
Treatment range: 4- 50 mg
Regimens: daily, 5 days daily and twice weekly after, 5 days daily and twice
weekly after, twice weekly and single dose

Uncertainty and Variability
(RSE, IIV, Shrinkage,
Bootstrap)

All model parameters were estimated
with good precision (RSE18.3%).
IIV was small to moderate for most of
the parameters and relatively high for
MAT. Shrinkage was low for most of
the parameters (30.7%) and
moderate for IIV on Vp/F. See

The number of post-first-dose
below limit of quantification (BLQ)
observations relative to the total
number of Vimseltinib plasma
observation records in the derived
data file was low and no alternative
modelling approaches for handling
these observations were deemed
necessary. These records were
excluded from model development.

model indicate a satisfactory
predictive performance for
vimseltinib plasma concentrations.

ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
See Figure 23 below for VPC.
Significant Covariates and
Clinical Relevance

Body weight and food status were
the most influential covariates on the
primary parameters in the final PK
model. Body weight was included
using allometric scaling with fixed
exponents, thus steady state
exposure increased with lower body
weight. The covariate-parameter
relation of food status on MAT
suggests that fed subjects had an
approximately 6-fold longer mean
absorption time than fasted subjects;
however, this had a limited effect on
steady state exposure metrics.
Several exploratory covariateparameter relationships were
identified during covariate analysis:
Healthy volunteers had a lower
Vc/F, and higher Vp/F, compared to
TGCT patients. Subjects of Black or
African American race had a higher
Vp/F compared to non-Black or
African American subjects.
However, the effect of all structural
and exploratory covariates on steady
state exposure parameters were
within the 80%-125% limits.

The FDA’s Assessment:

19.4.1.3.

PPK Review Issues

The impact of hepatic function on PK was evaluated using popPK analysis to support labelling.
There was no hepatic impairment other than mild by NCI ODWG classification in a total of 11
patients (7 with TGCT) from Ph1/2 study DCC-3014-01-001 and MOTION study. The mild
hepatic impairment was not identified as a significant covariate based on 159 observations from
these 11 patients. Despite small patient number, the number of observations and the coverage for
time after dose is considered adequate to characterize the effect of mild hepatic impairment on PK.
The baseline characteristics used in popPK analysis was provided in Table 57.

Reviewer’s Independent Analysis

None.
Exposure-Response Analysis
19.4.2.1.

ER (efficacy) Executive Summary

The FDA’s Assessment:
Lack of positive E-R relationships for efficacy endpoints was observed across steadystate exposure with a planned fixed dosage of 30 mg biw (median relative dose
intensity of 74%). Similar ORR was observed between patients with and without
dosage reduction that suggested the efficacy in patients who needed dose
modifications was not impacted as a result of dose reduction.
19.4.2.2.

ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
comments]
Model Structure

The final ORR per RECIST v1.1 model is a logistic
regression model with a linear effect of Cavg,ss, and a
linear effect of baseline tumor size. The final ORR per
TVS model is a logistic regression model with a linear
effect of Cavg,ss.

Yes

Model Parameter Estimates

ORR per RECIST v1.1: Table 58 below.
ORR per TVS: Table 59 below.
Model diagnostics for the final ORR per RECIST v1.1
and ORR per TVS models indicated a satisfactory
predictive performance for the observed data.
ORR per RECIST v1.1: A covariate effect of baseline
tumor size on ORR per RECIST v1.1 was included,
where lower baseline tumor size was associated with
higher ORR per RECIST v1.1.
ORR per TVS at Week 25: No covariates were
identified.
Following vimseltinib 30 mg twice weekly dosing,
ORR per RECIST v1.1 and ORR per TVS at Week 25
were predicted to be 41.4% and 68.5%, respectively
(Figure 24, below). The predicted proportions were
similar across body weight subgroups.
Proportion of responders versus Cavg,ss, is plotted for
the final ORR per RECIST v1.1 model, based on the
ORR per RECIST v1.1 analysis data set.in Figure 25
below.
Proportion of responders versus Cavg,ss is plotted for
the final ORR per TVS model, based on the ORR per
TVS analysis data set in Figure 26 below.
The available ORR per RECIST v1.1 and ORR per
TVS data showed that part of the TGCT patients
treated with vimseltinib responded to treatment, while
there were no responders in the placebo group. The ER
analyses did not show differences in response of ORR
per RECIST v1.1 and ORR per TVS at different levels
of exposure within the evaluated 30 mg BIW dose
group.

19.4.2.3.

ER (safety) Executive Summary

The FDA’s Assessment:
Positive E-R relationships was observed across steady-state exposure for periorbital
edema, rash, and AST/ALT elevation. The lower dosage (i.e., 20 mg biw) is predicted
to marginally improve safety.

Final Model Parameters

Model Structure

Model Parameter Estimates

For all safety endpoints, the final model was a
CPH model where the hazard for the event of
interest increased with increasing Cmax,ss (loglinear relationship). For AST elevation, an
additional parameter was included in the model,
describing the increased hazard in the placebo
treatment group, compared to the vimseltinib
treatment group, allowing for a better estimation of
the effect of Cmax,ss for this endpoint.

Model diagnostics for the final time to first event

Time to first event of periorbital edema: All
covariates except tumor type were not found to be
statistically significant. The probability of having a
periorbital edema event was decreased for subjects
with MST, compared to subjects with TGCT.

of sex and tumor type were found to be statistically

The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the E-R analysis conducted by Applicant.
19.4.2.5.

ER Review Issues

E-R relationship for CPK elevation
For CPK elevation that’s drug-related, the incidence is 24% of any Grade and 10% of Grade3
that led to dose reduction in 8.5% of TGCT patients in MOTION study. The E-R analysis was
therefore requested by the reviewer and the results were submitted as a response to Information
Request.
In Ph1/2 study DCC-3014-01-001 where CPK lab measurement was collected, similar
elevation of CPK over time was observed in 3 cohorts of TGCT patients (cohort 5: 30 mg biw,
cohort 8: 10 mg qd, cohort 9: 6 mg qd) (Figure 29). Notably, Cohort 5 and cohort 8 have
similar exposures despite different dosing intervals. E-R analysis for maximal CPK change
from baseline was evaluated using generalized linear model and non-linear models, and a
continuous model with linear effect of Cavg was selected as the base model. The effect of
Cavg,ss on maximal CPK change from baseline was found statistically insignificant (p-value
= 0.719) and associated with high uncertainty, suggesting a lack of E-R relationship (Table
64). In the covariate analysis, tumor type was identified as a significant covariate where
patients with TGCT were more likely to have greater CPK elevation compared to patients with
MST.
Pooled E-R analysis using data from both Ph1/2 and MOTION studies was conducted to
further evaluate if any Grade or Grade 3 CPK first elevation by CTCAE is dependent on
exposure using Cox proportional hazard model. While there was a significantly positive effect
of Cavg,ss for any Grade CPK first elevation (Table 65), the positive effect of Cavg,ss for
Grade 3 CPK first elevation was marginally non-significant (p=0.0758) (Table 66). For 20
mg biw dosage, the predicted proportion of events at week 25 was only slightly lower than 30
mg biw dosage.
Compared to the 10 mg QD dose, the 30 mg BIW dose resulted in a 14% lower Cavg,ss and a
7% higher Cmax,ss. The predicted proportion of safety events was similar between the 10 mg
QD and 30 mg BIW doses. However, it remains unclear whether a sustained higher Cavg/Cmin
of 10 mg QD could lead to additional chronic safety concerns for QD dosing regimen.

19.4.2.6.

Reviewer’s Independent Analysis

Efficacy with a high rate of dose reduction
Given AE leading to dose reduction occurred in 39% of patients early, relative to the median time
to response, the impact on efficacy was evaluated. ORR by RECIST/TVS between patients with
and without dose reduction at any time during treatment was compared, which showed similar %
of responders (Table 67). Notably, the steady-state exposures in patients with dose reduction were
higher than those without dose reduction, indicating dose modifications are needed to mitigate
exposure driven toxicities. Considering these toxicities are generally reversible, the proposed
dosage is considered acceptable.

19.4.2.7.

Overall benefit-risk evaluation based on E-R analyses

The Applicant’s Position:
An exposure efficacy analysis based on data from participants with TGCT who were
randomized to receive 30 mg twice weekly dosing regimen in MOTION did not show
association between vimseltinib exposure and ORR per RECIST v1.1 or TVS. A flat
exposure-efficacy relationship was identified across the exposure range for the
evaluated dosing regimen. The exposure-efficacy model could not predict ORR per
RECIST v1.1 or TVS outside of the 30 mg twice weekly dosing regimen, as a
substantial part of the simulated exposure distribution would be outside of the
evaluated exposure range.
An exposure safety analysis including data from participants with TGCT who were
randomized to receive 30 mg twice weekly dosing regimen in MOTION showed a
positive and mostly shallow relationship between vimseltinib exposure and safety
(periorbital edema, rash, ALT, and AST). Predictions from exposure-safety models
showed that lower vimseltinib exposure may decrease the probability of safety events
in some participants. For periorbital edema, rash, and ALT elevation, the difference in
the median predicted proportions of these safety events at Week 25 was marginal at
<5% following 20 mg twice weekly, compared to 30 mg twice weekly. For AST

ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
19.4.3.2.

Background

Vimseltinib, also known as DCC-3014 and DP-6865, is a kinase inhibitor of the colonystimulating factor 1 receptor (CSF1R) kinase currently being developed for treatment of adult
patients with symptomatic tenosynovial giant cell tumor (TGCT). The available dose strengths
are 14, 20 and 30 mg capsules. The recommended dosing regimen is 30 mg administered orally
twice weekly at least three days apart. The tablets should be swallowed as a whole and can be
taken with or without food.
Following oral administration of single doses of vimseltinib 6 to 50 mg in healthy
subjects, vimseltinib Cmax and AUC0-24h were increased dose proportionally (DCC-3014-01-002).
In patients with TGCT (Cohort 5 of Study DCC-3014-01-001), exposure on Cycle 2 Day 1
following twice weekly 30 mg doses was approximately 3.6-fold higher than exposure following
a single 30 mg dose (Cycle 1 Day 1) (). Steady state was reached following approximately 5
weeks of 30 mg vimseltinib twice weekly administration (DCC-3014-01-001). Following
administration of a single 30 mg vimseltinib with a standardized FDA high-fat meal in healthy
subjects, the Cmax of vimseltinib decreased approximately 30%, compared to the fasted state, and
Tmax was delayed by 5 hours. Its AUC0-inf was comparable under the fasted and fed conditions
(DCC-3014-01-002).
Vimseltinib has high permeability and pH-dependent solubility. It is well-absorbed and is
extensively metabolized primarily via oxidation, N-demethylation, and N-dealkylation (DCC3014-03-0023). The enzymes involved in the metabolism of vimseltinib were not identified
when parent disappearance was monitored for the reaction phenotyping studies conducted using
human liver microsomes and recombinant CYP enzymes due to low turnover of vimseltinib
(DCC-3014-03-0007). In the human ADME study, approximately 37.5% and 42.9% of
radioactivity were recovered in the urine (5.1% unchanged parent) and the feces (9.1%
unchanged parent), respectively (DCC-3014-01-003).
Vimseltinib is not a competitive or time-dependent inhibitor of any of the commonly
studied CYP enzymes (DCC-3014-03-0001). Vimseltinib is not an inducer of CYP1A2 and
CYP3A4. Although dose-dependent induction of CYP2B6 was observed, the fold induction of
CYP2B6 mRNA and activity was below 2-fold at the vimseltinib concentration that was 50 times
of its unbound steady-state Cmax (DCC-3014-03-0009). Vimseltinib is a substrate of P-gp (DCC3014-03-0006). Vimseltinib inhibits P-gp (IC50= 4.35 PM), BCRP (IC50= 0.556 PM), OATP1B3
(IC50= 10.1 PM), OATP1B1 (IC50= 9.21 PM), MATE1 (IC50= 7.58 PM), MATE2K (IC50= 23.3
PM), OAT1 (IC50= 51.5 PM), OAT2 (IC50= 23.7 PM), and OCT2 (IC50= 0.456 PM) (DCC-301403-0006, DCC-3014-03-0024). The applicant conducted clinical DDI studies with itraconazole,
and rabeprazole to evaluate some of the in vitro findings. Refer to the Clinical Pharmacology
review section for detail information on vimseltinib regarding ADME properties, in vitro and
clinical studies used in PBPK modeling.

Methods

Simulations related to evaluation of vimseltinib as an inhibitor of transporters were performed
using the PK/PD Profiles mode in the Simcyp® Simulator (Version 22 Certara, Sheffield, UK).
Schemes of the PBPK simulation strategy are shown in Figure 30, which summarizes the studies
used for vimseltinib model development and verification, and model applications in DDI
predictions. The final model input parameters were summarized in Table 68. The vimseltinib
PBPK model consists of an ADAM absorption model, a full PBPK model for distribution and an
enzyme kinetics model for elimination. The Simcyp library files itraconazole_Fasted soln, SV(b) (4)

1. Can the PBPK model adequately describe the PK profiles of vimseltinib?

Effects of vimseltinib on the P-gp substrate dabigatran etexilate
Dabigatran etexilate is a P-gp substrate. Dabigatran is a metabolite of dabigatran etexilate and is
not a P-gp substrate. The effects of P-gp inhibitors on dabigatran etexilate are assessed by
changes in dabigatran plasma exposure. The ability of the PBPK models of dabigatran etexilate
and dabigatran to predict P-gp interaction was assessed using clinical DDIs of dabigatran with
verapamil, clarithromycin, quinidine, and ketoconazole (Dabigatran Simcyp summary report). Of
note, the ketoconazole DDI data was also used in the model development. Large range of IC50
values of these inhibitors are reported in the Certara DDI Database. The difference in IC50
between minimum and maximum values of each inhibitor ranges from 22- to 7450-fold if all the
results were considered, and 1.5- to 280-fold if only experiments conducted in the condition
(b) (4)
similar to that of vimseltinib (e.g. Caco-2 cells and
as a probe substrate) were
(b) (4)
considered. In addition, the reviewer

The applicant was requested to simulate the effects of vimseltinib on dabigatran exposure
when the P-gp Ki was 50-, 100-, and 200-fold lower than the in vitro IC50, and the dose
staggering scheme under the above conditions. The results are shown in Figure 33 When
vimseltinib and dabigatran etexilate were administered simultaneously and considering a P-gp Ki
value 200-fold lower than the in vitro IC50 value, vimseltinib was predicted to increase
dabigatran AUC and Cmax by approximately 2.6- and 3.0-fold, respectively, which were
approximately 2-fold higher than that predicted using the Ki value calculated from the in vitro
IC50 value using the Cheng-Prusoff equation (PMID: 4202581). In addition, the reviewer also
simulated the effects of vimseltinib on dabigatran exposure by reducing the P-gp IC50 value by
1000-fold. Vimseltinib was predicted to increase both dabigatran AUC and Cmax by
approximately 3.0-fold. When dabigatran etexilate was administered 4 hours after vimseltinib
dosing, the inhibitory effects of vimseltinib on dabigatran exposure were diminished (e.g.
increases in AUC and Cmax were approximately 1.25-fold) regardless of the Ki value used in the
simulations. Therefore, despite uncertainties in the predicted interaction effect of vimseltinib, the
potential risk for a clinically significant interaction between vimseltinib and the P-gp substrate
dabigatran etexilate may be mitigated via dose staggering.

ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
Figure 33

Effects of vimseltinib on dabigatran exposure following staggered dosing

Ten virtual trials of 10 patients with cancer (50% female) aged 20 to 50 years receiving 150 mg dabigatran etexilate
(P-gp probe substrate) as a single dose with and without co-administration of vimseltinib (30 mg given every 84
hours for a total of 46 days) were generated. All simulations were performed under steady-state conditions for
vimseltinib. Staggering simulations were performed in which the dose of dabigatran etexilate was administered 0 to
4 hours after the last dose of vimseltinib (30 mg every 84 hours for 46 days). Ki values that were 50-, 100-, 200- and
1000-fold lower than in vitro IC50 value for P-gp were applied in these simulations. The simulated geometric mean
AUCinf and Cmax values and corresponding GMRs for dabigatran with and without coadministration of vimseltinib
are presented.
Source: Table 10 in clin-response-ir13-20240910

Effects of vimseltinib on the OCT2 substrate

OCT2 inhibition is considered inadequate.

Conclusions

x

PBPK analysis was considered adequate to inform the interaction potential between
vimseltinib and the P-gp substrate dabigatran. Vimseltinib was predicted to increase
dabigatran AUC and Cmax 2- to 3.0-fold when administered simultaneously and
considering the uncertainty on the in vitro generated IC50 of P-gp,. However, dabigatran
AUC and Cmax were predicted to increase up to 1.3-fold when dabigatran etexilate was
administered 4 hours after vimseltinib dosing regardless of the Ki value used in the
simulations. Therefore, despite uncertainties in the predicted effect, the potential risk for
a clinically significant interaction between vimseltinib and the P-gp substrate dabigatran
(b) (4)
etexilate may be mitigated via dose staggering.

x

Vimseltinib was predicted to increase rosuvastatin AUC and Cmax up to 2.6- and 6.3-fold,
respectively when simultaneously administered and considering the uncertainty on the in
(b) (4)
vitro generated IC50 of BCRP.

Determination of DCC-3014 and DP-7005 in Human K2-EDTA Plasma by LCMS/MS ( (b) (4) 1100004). This method underwent three amendments.
x Validation amendment report No.1: Partial Validation ( (b) (4) 1100004_am1).
This amendment extended the calibration standards for DP-7005 only.
x Validation amendment report No.2: Additional Long-Term Stability, Cross
Validation, and Interference Check ( (b) (4) 1100004 am2). This amendment
was created to report long-term stability in human K2-EDTA plasma for
DCC-3014 stored for 372 days at -70°C and stored for 525 days at -20°C.
Additionally, the assay procedure underwent cross validation for laboratory
relocation, meeting the established acceptance criteria. This amendment also
reported the interference evaluation of the samples, for each analyte at the
concentration of the LLOQ calibration standard, in the presence of
itraconazole and hydroxyitraconazole, each at about 3000 ng/mL, and
rabeprazole at about 1000 ng/mL.
